Lesson 05: Separating requested extras from agreed work
Original fictional case: At a bedroom remodel, carpenter Jules reviews the scope with client Amara. The agreed work is replacing skirting only. Decorating is excluded. Amara asks for a built-in bench as an extra. Jules can arrange a separate quotation, but no bench dimensions, price, or completion date are agreed. The conversation must distinguish the existing trim work from the requested bench and must not imply that decorating or a free extra is included.
Language workshop: A bench beyond the skirting scope
Communication goal: Explain the agreed task, clarify an additional request, and discuss a separate quotation without implying approval or completion timing.
Language guidance: Replacing the bedroom skirting is the only agreed task. The bench is an additional request, and decorating is excluded. A separate quotation can be arranged, but the request itself does not establish bench measurements, an accepted price, or a completion date.
Useful expressions (preserve the supplied facts): I understand you would like a built-in bench as well. / The current scope is replacing the bedroom skirting only. / Decorating is excluded from this booking. / The bench is additional work, not part of the existing task. / I can arrange a separate quotation. / We have not agreed the bench dimensions. / There is no agreed bench price yet. / No completion date is confirmed for the bench. / I cannot treat the bench as a free addition. / A bench request does not add painting to the current scope. / We need a defined bench request for the quotation. / Asking for a quote does not approve the extra work. / The skirting replacement remains the agreed task. / I will not put guessed measurements into the handoff. / Built-in bench requested; dimensions, price, and timing unresolved. / I will pass the bench request for a separate quotation.
Vocabulary: skirting: Trim along the base of an internal wall, often called baseboard. / baseboard: Common North American term for trim at the foot of an internal wall. / built-in bench: Fixed seating integrated into a room or fitted assembly. / agreed scope: Work explicitly included in the current agreement. / scope exclusion: Work or condition left outside the agreement. / decorating: Surface finishing work such as painting or wallpapering. / additional work: Task requested beyond the existing scope. / separate quotation: Distinct price proposal for specified extra work. / bench dimensions: Measurements defining the proposed bench. / design brief: Statement of the requirements for a proposed design. / seat height: Vertical position of a seat surface from a stated reference. / seat depth: Front-to-back dimension of a seat surface. / overall length: Full end-to-end measurement of an item. / finish requirement: Specified surface appearance or treatment. / quotation scope: Tasks and terms covered by a price proposal. / variation: Agreed or proposed change to the original work arrangement. / change authorization: Permission to alter the agreed work. / price agreement: Acceptance of a stated cost for defined work. / completion commitment: Agreed promise about when work will finish. / book matching: Pairing consecutive veneer leaves by alternating their faces for mirrored grain. / measurement visit: Attendance to establish relevant dimensions. / client approval: Acceptance from the client through the relevant process. / existing contract: Current agreement governing the booked work. / scope handoff: Transfer of the task description for further review or quotation.
Speaking task: Read turns 1-10; separate the existing skirting agreement from the proposed bench and decorating. Switch roles for turns 11-20. Stress quotation, authorization, and unresolved timing. Check and read the transfer, keeping the quote request separate from permission to begin.
Partner: Amara: Client / Jules: Carpenter
Guided response task: Select supplied answers for the dialogue gaps. Check the explanations, then read the completed exchange aloud; no original writing is required.
Optional second-round challenge: Complete the client exchange about the bedroom work. Preserve the original task, excluded decorating, separate quotation, and unresolved bench dimensions.
Model for comparison AFTER my attempt, not a response to give me first: Amara: Good. Please don't take my interest as approval. I'll review the defined proposal and price before giving a decision on the extra work.
Jules: Correct. Change authorization has not happened in this conversation. I will not describe your interest as approval to start building or to choose measurements for you.
Amara: Please pass on: built-in bedroom bench requested for a separate quote; size, finish, price, and timing unresolved. No instruction to proceed.
Jules: I will make that scope handoff clearly. The note will keep the bench proposal separate, preserve the decorating exclusion, and leave the unagreed details open for the quotation process.
END REFERENCE
Rehearse with an AI colleague — complete prompt

Copy the full text below into your preferred AI. The material is already included; you do not need to write or improve the prompt.

TASK: Interactive workplace role-play
1. Use the supplied case or dialogue as the starting situation. Give a two-sentence briefing and offer two relevant professional roles for me to choose from. For a supplied dialogue, use its actual roles. Ask which role I want, then stop and wait. Do not write my replies.
2. After I choose, identify who you will play and the immediate communication goal. Play the other professional; where a meeting requires a third person, label each of your speakers clearly. Start with one natural workplace turn and wait for my reply. Keep most turns to one to three sentences and ask no more than one question at a time.
3. Let the exchange develop across six to ten learner turns, or end earlier if I type "feedback". Respond to what I actually say. Include a plausible clarification, disagreement or tradeoff without silently changing the starting facts. Mark any added constraint as a fictional second-round variation. Do not resolve approvals, evidence or commitments that remain uncertain.
4. Stay in role during the exchange. If my meaning is unclear, ask for clarification naturally. Give a hint only if I ask or cannot proceed. Do not deliver a model conversation in advance.
5. At the debrief, quote two of my phrases: one successful choice and one worth improving. Check factual accuracy, language, register and whether the next step was clear. Give up to three focused suggestions. Ask me to retry the weakest turn; wait. Only then offer an alternative wording and a harder replay.
Use voice replies when available. If I prefer selections, offer four plausible spoken replies and explain my choice before continuing. Do not require written compositions.

06Coordinating access and other tradesDelivery confirmed, study access unresolved+

Your goal

Report delivery, room access, review timing, and storage restrictions as separate arrangements when coordinating with other trades.

This lesson in the book, page 49 ↗

A Read the situation

Delivery confirmed, study access unresolved

Carpenter Ben updates site coordinator Farah on the study joinery. Delivery is confirmed for Tuesday morning, but the decorator has not released the study for carpentry access. The hallway is available for a separate trim review at 11:00, not for material storage. Farah needs a precise status report. Ben must not turn the delivery confirmation into installation access, or the hallway review arrangement into permission to leave delivered joinery there.

Farah: Site coordinator / Ben: Carpenter

Check the facts · 3 questions

Choose the answer supported by the briefing. Do not assume that a proposed action has already happened.

1. What is confirmed?

Answer and explanation

A. Joinery delivery for Tuesday morning

Only the delivery arrangement is confirmed for Tuesday morning.

2. What remains unresolved?

Answer and explanation

D. The decorator has not released the study for carpentry access

Study access is unresolved because the decorator has not released that area.

3. What is the hallway available for?

Answer and explanation

B. A separate trim review at 11:00, not storage

The hallway permission is specific to the trim review and excludes material storage.

B Find the words

Vocabulary and collocations

Tuesday morning belongs to the confirmed delivery. Study access is not released. The 11:00 hallway arrangement is for a trim review only, and hallway storage is not permitted. These are separate facts, not a single approval for delivery, storage, and installation.

24 field terms and natural word combinations
joinery
Fitted woodwork or related manufactured components for a project.coordinate the joinery delivery
delivery slot
Agreed time period for receiving goods.confirm the delivery slot
trade coordination
Arrangement of work between different specialist teams.improve trade coordination
access release
Confirmation that a defined area is available for the specified work.request an access release
decorator
Trade worker responsible for agreed surface finishing work.coordinate with the decorator
carpentry access
Permission and readiness to enter an area for carpentry work.confirm carpentry access
work area
Location assigned for a particular task.identify the work area
trim review
Check or discussion of the specified trim details.attend the trim review
storage permission
Authorization to leave materials in a defined location.confirm storage permission
material staging
Temporary positioning of materials before use or movement.distinguish material staging from a review
site coordinator
Person coordinating site arrangements across people or tasks.update the site coordinator
dependency
Condition that another action relies on.identify the access dependency
area readiness
Whether a location is prepared for the intended activity.confirm area readiness
handover between trades
Transfer of an area or task from one trade to another.coordinate the handover between trades
installation access
Availability of an area for fitting work.verify installation access
confirmed delivery
Agreed arrival arrangement for goods.report the confirmed delivery
moisture content
Water mass expressed here as a percentage of oven-dry wood mass.report wood moisture content
purpose-specific access
Permission limited to a named activity.respect purpose-specific access
equilibrium moisture content
Wood moisture at balance with surrounding temperature and humidity.distinguish equilibrium moisture content
logistics update
Report on movement, receipt, access, or related arrangements.give a logistics update
storage location
Place where materials may be held.confirm the storage location
schedule dependency
Arrangement that must be resolved for a later task to proceed.report a schedule dependency
status readback
Repetition of key arrangements to check understanding.provide a status readback
coordination query
Question seeking clarification of interdependent arrangements.raise a coordination query

The scenario does not establish a substitute storage location, a new delivery slot, or the time when the decorator will release the study. Report the unresolved dependency to Farah without inventing a workaround or blaming another trade for an unverified delay.

C Notice the language

Coordinate by purpose, not just by place and time

A room can be available for one activity and unavailable for another. State the purpose attached to each arrangement. Trade coordination works best when delivery, access, review, and storage are reported separately, with the unresolved dependency visible.

16 useful phrases, grouped by purpose
Start with delivery
The joinery delivery is confirmed for Tuesday morning.
Separate the room status
The study has not been released for carpentry access.
Name the dependency
We still need the decorator's access release.
Avoid merging permissions
Delivery confirmation does not establish installation access.
State the review arrangement
The hallway is available for the trim review at 11:00.
State the restriction
The hallway is not available for material storage.
Clarify the purpose
That access is for a review, not for staging the delivery.
Keep an alternative open
No alternative storage location is confirmed here.
Avoid changing the booking
I have not changed the delivery slot.
Avoid guessing readiness
I do not have a confirmed study-release time.
Preserve trade coordination
Please keep the access question visible in the coordination update.
Avoid assigning blame
I am reporting the release status, not a cause of delay.
Read back the arrangements
Tuesday delivery, study unreleased, 11:00 hallway review, no hallway storage.
Ask for clarification
Can you coordinate the unresolved access arrangement?
Keep installation separate
No installation start is established by this update.
Close with the open item
Study access remains the unresolved dependency.
6 language and precision notes
Confirmed for
Attaches a definite arrangement to its specific purpose.
Released for
Refers to authorization or readiness for the named work, not every use of the room.
Available for review
Does not imply permission to store materials in the same location.
Not for storage
An explicit restriction that must remain in the handoff.
Still need
Flags a dependency without inventing its cause or resolution.
Separate
Prevents one agreed arrangement from silently authorizing another.

D Check your understanding

Choose the precise message

Choose one best answer using the case facts and the stated purpose.

1. Which status report is accurate?

Answer and explanation

C. Tuesday-morning delivery confirmed; study access unreleased; hallway review at 11:00; no hallway storage.

The report preserves each arrangement and the specific hallway restriction.

2. Which inference is unsupported?

Answer and explanation

B. A confirmed delivery means the study is ready for installation.

Delivery and room access are separate arrangements, so one does not prove the other.

3. What can Farah coordinate next?

Answer and explanation

A. The unresolved study-access arrangement

Farah needs the unresolved access dependency, not an unauthorized substitute arrangement.

4. Which hallway statement should be corrected?

Answer and explanation

C. We can store the joinery there because the trim review is allowed.

Permission for the review does not authorize material storage in the hallway.

E Conversations

Delivery confirmed, study access unresolved

Extended conversation · 20 turns · 10 gaps

Farah: Site coordinator / Ben: Carpenter

Complete the numbered gaps. Use each bank entry once, without changing its form.

Word bank
  • Purpose-specific access
  • access release
  • trim review
  • schedule dependency
  • logistics update
  • confirmed delivery
  • status readback
  • storage location
  • delivery slot
  • Installation access
  1. Farah
    Ben, I have Tuesday delivery in the diary and an eleven-o'clock review. Does that mean the study will be ready to install, or am I combining separate arrangements?
  2. Ben
    The 1 is confirmed for Tuesday morning. The study, however, has not been released by the decorator for carpentry access.
  3. Farah
    Is the decorator finished with the study? I don't want the delivery confirmation to be read as permission for the installers to enter that room.
  4. Ben
    No confirmed 2 time is available. I want that dependency kept visible rather than treated as settled in the delivery update.
  5. Farah
    Then what exactly is booked for eleven in the hallway? The word access in the message is a bit too broad for me.
  6. Ben
    The hallway is available for a 3 at 11:00. That is a separate activity, and the hallway is not available for material storage.
  7. Farah
    Can the joinery wait in the hallway until the study is released? I'm asking before anyone puts that location on the delivery instructions.
  8. Ben
    No alternative 4 is confirmed here. I should not invent one or assume that a room available for a discussion can receive the delivery.
  9. Farah
    So the delivery is still Tuesday morning, even though we don't yet have a permitted storage arrangement? Please don't invent a new slot in the update.
  10. Ben
    I have not changed the 5. Tuesday morning is still the stated slot. The update flags the mismatch without claiming that a replacement plan exists.
  11. Farah
    And we can't advertise Tuesday installation just because the material is arriving? The room release is still the missing part.
  12. Ben
    No. 6 remains unresolved. Delivery, room release, and the start of fitting work should not be compressed into one promise.
  13. Farah
    Please put that distinction in the coordination message. What would you say without blaming the decorator for a delay we haven't established?
  14. Ben
    That is the right 7 to give. I am reporting the current release status, not a reason for it or a claim that another trade has missed a deadline.
  15. Farah
    Read the four statuses back separately, please. I want delivery, study access, review access, and storage to be unmistakable.
  16. Ben
    Here is the 8: Tuesday-morning delivery confirmed; study unreleased; hallway trim review at 11:00; no material storage in the hallway.
  17. Farah
    Do we know when the study will be released? If not, keep that unknown rather than using eleven o'clock as a convenient estimate.
  18. Ben
    Yes. The 9 is still the study release. No alternative storage, changed delivery slot, or installation start has been established in this update.
  19. Farah
    All right. Please flag the unresolved coordination to me. The hallway review permission mustn't turn into a general storage or installation permission.
  20. Ben
    Exactly. 10 is the key point for the hallway. I will keep the review permission and the storage restriction together whenever I pass the update on.

Answers and explanations
  1. delivery slot Turn 2. Delivery slot is Tuesday morning for the joinery; it does not establish room access, storage permission, or installation readiness.
  2. access release Turn 4. Access release for the study has not been given, and no release time is supplied in the case.
  3. trim review Turn 6. Trim review is the specific activity allowed in the hallway at 11:00; it is not a material-storage arrangement.
  4. storage location Turn 8. Storage location is not confirmed elsewhere, and the hallway is explicitly excluded from material storage.
  5. confirmed delivery Turn 10. Confirmed delivery remains Tuesday morning; reporting the access conflict does not itself reschedule the supplier.
  6. Installation access Turn 12. Installation access is unresolved because the study has not been released; a delivered product does not authorize fitting work there.
  7. logistics update Turn 14. Logistics update reports the actual arrangements and unresolved dependency without inventing a cause or blaming another trade.
  8. status readback Turn 16. Status readback repeats delivery, access, review, and storage separately so no permission is lost or expanded in the handoff.
  9. schedule dependency Turn 18. Schedule dependency is the unresolved study access, which remains distinct from the already confirmed delivery and review arrangements.
  10. Purpose-specific access Turn 20. Purpose-specific access limits the hallway arrangement to the trim review; it does not authorize storage or substitute for study access.
Read the complete conversation
  1. Farah: Ben, I have Tuesday delivery in the diary and an eleven-o'clock review. Does that mean the study will be ready to install, or am I combining separate arrangements?
  2. Ben: The delivery slot is confirmed for Tuesday morning. The study, however, has not been released by the decorator for carpentry access.
  3. Farah: Is the decorator finished with the study? I don't want the delivery confirmation to be read as permission for the installers to enter that room.
  4. Ben: No confirmed access release time is available. I want that dependency kept visible rather than treated as settled in the delivery update.
  5. Farah: Then what exactly is booked for eleven in the hallway? The word access in the message is a bit too broad for me.
  6. Ben: The hallway is available for a trim review at 11:00. That is a separate activity, and the hallway is not available for material storage.
  7. Farah: Can the joinery wait in the hallway until the study is released? I'm asking before anyone puts that location on the delivery instructions.
  8. Ben: No alternative storage location is confirmed here. I should not invent one or assume that a room available for a discussion can receive the delivery.
  9. Farah: So the delivery is still Tuesday morning, even though we don't yet have a permitted storage arrangement? Please don't invent a new slot in the update.
  10. Ben: I have not changed the confirmed delivery. Tuesday morning is still the stated slot. The update flags the mismatch without claiming that a replacement plan exists.
  11. Farah: And we can't advertise Tuesday installation just because the material is arriving? The room release is still the missing part.
  12. Ben: No. Installation access remains unresolved. Delivery, room release, and the start of fitting work should not be compressed into one promise.
  13. Farah: Please put that distinction in the coordination message. What would you say without blaming the decorator for a delay we haven't established?
  14. Ben: That is the right logistics update to give. I am reporting the current release status, not a reason for it or a claim that another trade has missed a deadline.
  15. Farah: Read the four statuses back separately, please. I want delivery, study access, review access, and storage to be unmistakable.
  16. Ben: Here is the status readback: Tuesday-morning delivery confirmed; study unreleased; hallway trim review at 11:00; no material storage in the hallway.
  17. Farah: Do we know when the study will be released? If not, keep that unknown rather than using eleven o'clock as a convenient estimate.
  18. Ben: Yes. The schedule dependency is still the study release. No alternative storage, changed delivery slot, or installation start has been established in this update.
  19. Farah: All right. Please flag the unresolved coordination to me. The hallway review permission mustn't turn into a general storage or installation permission.
  20. Ben: Exactly. Purpose-specific access is the key point for the hallway. I will keep the review permission and the storage restriction together whenever I pass the update on.
Dialogue in the book, page 55 ↗
Three more conversations at work

Each short conversation has its own fictional situation and facts.

Delivery is ready, the room is not

A carpenter updates the site coordinator about Tuesday's joinery.

  1. Carpenter: Tuesday's joinery delivery is confirmed, but the decorator hasn't released the study yet.
  2. Site coordinator: Could the crew use the hallway while we sort out access to the study?
  3. Carpenter: The hallway window is for an eleven o'clock trim review, not storage or fitting.
  4. Site coordinator: Thanks for separating those. I'll ask the decorator when the study can be released.
  5. Carpenter: I'll check delivery flexibility with the supplier rather than assume materials can stay elsewhere.
  6. Site coordinator: Please don't cancel the delivery until we know both options and their consequences.
  7. Carpenter: Agreed. I'll ask what's possible and bring the response back before changing anything.
  8. Site coordinator: The decorator has just offered Wednesday morning. Does that resolve the whole sequence?
  9. Carpenter: It resolves the proposed access window, but supplier timing and crew availability still need checking.
  10. Site coordinator: I'll show Wednesday as proposed in the lookahead schedule, with those two dependencies open.
The countertop appointment moves

A carpenter and kitchen coordinator discuss a revised supplier visit.

  1. Carpenter: The countertop surveyor moved their appointment to Thursday. Does the current cabinet review still work?
  2. Kitchen coordinator: Our cabinet review was Friday, so the surveyor would arrive before that review.
  3. Carpenter: Then we need to reconcile the sequence rather than tell both teams they're confirmed.
  4. Kitchen coordinator: Can your review move to Wednesday afternoon, or is that still unconfirmed?
  5. Carpenter: Wednesday is possible for me, but the client needs to attend the appearance review.
  6. Kitchen coordinator: I'll ask the client about Wednesday and explain why the timing matters for Thursday.
  7. Carpenter: Please distinguish the appearance review from the surveyor's own measurement and acceptance requirements.
  8. Kitchen coordinator: I will. Moving our meeting doesn't automatically mean their survey prerequisites are satisfied.
  9. Carpenter: Once both parties confirm their requirements, we can update the lookahead with real dependencies.
  10. Kitchen coordinator: I'll contact them today and keep Thursday tentative until the sequence is agreed.
The retained floor needs a clear boundary

A carpenter and homeowner discuss access through an occupied room.

  1. Carpenter: Our agreed route passes through the sitting room. Which furniture will remain in place?
  2. Homeowner: The piano stays, and the desk must remain usable during my afternoon appointments.
  3. Carpenter: Then we need the protection plan and access window to reflect both constraints.
  4. Homeowner: Can your crew simply use the side doorway whenever the front room is busy?
  5. Carpenter: That route wasn't reviewed. I'll ask the site coordinator to assess it before agreeing.
  6. Homeowner: Please explain that I need quiet from one until three, not all afternoon.
  7. Carpenter: I'll state those hours precisely and ask whether the planned sequence can accommodate them.
  8. Homeowner: That sounds workable. Can we have one contact for confirming any daily access changes?
  9. Carpenter: The site coordinator can handle those confirmations; I'll make sure your constraint reaches them.
  10. Homeowner: Thank you. I can plan my appointments around a confirmed window, not an informal guess.

F Say it

Two scenarios to practice

Delivery confirmed, study access unresolved
  1. Read turns 1-10 with a partner, distinguishing Tuesday delivery from study access and the hallway review.
  2. Switch roles for turns 11-20. Read the four statuses without adding a storage location or installation time.

On your own: read both roles. With a partner: swap roles after the first reading. Use the completed conversation to check any line you need to repeat.

Keep four arrangements separate

Complete the coordinator update. Preserve the confirmed delivery, unreleased study, hallway review time, and storage restriction.

Choose one reply for each gap, then read the four turns in order. Check the explanation after each choice.

1. Carpenter: "Delivery is confirmed for ___ morning."

Answer and explanation

B. Tuesday

Tuesday morning is the delivery slot, not a confirmed installation start.

2. Coordinator: "The study has not been ___ for carpentry access."

Answer and explanation

D. released

The decorator has not released the study for the intended work.

3. Carpenter: "The hallway trim review is at ___."

Answer and explanation

A. 11:00

Eleven o'clock is the separate trim-review time supplied in the case.

4. Coordinator: "The hallway is not available for material ___."

Answer and explanation

B. storage

Storage is explicitly excluded even though the hallway review is allowed.

Read the completed exchange twice, switching roles. Keep the printed facts and professional responsibilities unchanged.

Lesson 06: Coordinating access and other trades
Original fictional case: Carpenter Ben updates site coordinator Farah on the study joinery. Delivery is confirmed for Tuesday morning, but the decorator has not released the study for carpentry access. The hallway is available for a separate trim review at 11:00, not for material storage. Farah needs a precise status report. Ben must not turn the delivery confirmation into installation access, or the hallway review arrangement into permission to leave delivered joinery there.
Language workshop: Delivery confirmed, study access unresolved
Communication goal: Report delivery, room access, review timing, and storage restrictions as separate arrangements when coordinating with other trades.
Language guidance: Tuesday morning belongs to the confirmed delivery. Study access is not released. The 11:00 hallway arrangement is for a trim review only, and hallway storage is not permitted. These are separate facts, not a single approval for delivery, storage, and installation.
Useful expressions (preserve the supplied facts): The joinery delivery is confirmed for Tuesday morning. / The study has not been released for carpentry access. / We still need the decorator's access release. / Delivery confirmation does not establish installation access. / The hallway is available for the trim review at 11:00. / The hallway is not available for material storage. / That access is for a review, not for staging the delivery. / No alternative storage location is confirmed here. / I have not changed the delivery slot. / I do not have a confirmed study-release time. / Please keep the access question visible in the coordination update. / I am reporting the release status, not a cause of delay. / Tuesday delivery, study unreleased, 11:00 hallway review, no hallway storage. / Can you coordinate the unresolved access arrangement? / No installation start is established by this update. / Study access remains the unresolved dependency.
Vocabulary: joinery: Fitted woodwork or related manufactured components for a project. / delivery slot: Agreed time period for receiving goods. / trade coordination: Arrangement of work between different specialist teams. / access release: Confirmation that a defined area is available for the specified work. / decorator: Trade worker responsible for agreed surface finishing work. / carpentry access: Permission and readiness to enter an area for carpentry work. / work area: Location assigned for a particular task. / trim review: Check or discussion of the specified trim details. / storage permission: Authorization to leave materials in a defined location. / material staging: Temporary positioning of materials before use or movement. / site coordinator: Person coordinating site arrangements across people or tasks. / dependency: Condition that another action relies on. / area readiness: Whether a location is prepared for the intended activity. / handover between trades: Transfer of an area or task from one trade to another. / installation access: Availability of an area for fitting work. / confirmed delivery: Agreed arrival arrangement for goods. / moisture content: Water mass expressed here as a percentage of oven-dry wood mass. / purpose-specific access: Permission limited to a named activity. / equilibrium moisture content: Wood moisture at balance with surrounding temperature and humidity. / logistics update: Report on movement, receipt, access, or related arrangements. / storage location: Place where materials may be held. / schedule dependency: Arrangement that must be resolved for a later task to proceed. / status readback: Repetition of key arrangements to check understanding. / coordination query: Question seeking clarification of interdependent arrangements.
Speaking task: Read turns 1-10 with a partner, distinguishing Tuesday delivery from study access and the hallway review. Switch roles for turns 11-20. Read the four statuses without adding a storage location or installation time. Check and read the transfer. Keep 11:00 attached to the review only.

Farah: All right. Please flag the unresolved coordination to me. The hallway review permission mustn't turn into a general storage or installation permission.

Rehearse with an AI colleague — complete prompt

Copy the full text below into your preferred AI. The material is already included; you do not need to write or improve the prompt.

TASK: Interactive workplace role-play
1. Use the supplied case or dialogue as the starting situation. Give a two-sentence briefing and offer two relevant professional roles for me to choose from. For a supplied dialogue, use its actual roles. Ask which role I want, then stop and wait. Do not write my replies.
2. After I choose, identify who you will play and the immediate communication goal. Play the other professional; where a meeting requires a third person, label each of your speakers clearly. Start with one natural workplace turn and wait for my reply. Keep most turns to one to three sentences and ask no more than one question at a time.
3. Let the exchange develop across six to ten learner turns, or end earlier if I type "feedback". Respond to what I actually say. Include a plausible clarification, disagreement or tradeoff without silently changing the starting facts. Mark any added constraint as a fictional second-round variation. Do not resolve approvals, evidence or commitments that remain uncertain.
4. Stay in role during the exchange. If my meaning is unclear, ask for clarification naturally. Give a hint only if I ask or cannot proceed. Do not deliver a model conversation in advance.
5. At the debrief, quote two of my phrases: one successful choice and one worth improving. Check factual accuracy, language, register and whether the next step was clear. Give up to three focused suggestions. Ask me to retry the weakest turn; wait. Only then offer an alternative wording and a harder replay.
Use voice replies when available. If I prefer selections, offer four plausible spoken replies and explain my choice before continuing. Do not require written compositions.

07Reviewing fit, appearance, and snaggingCorrecting the claim about drawer D3+

Your goal

Withdraw an inaccurate overall claim, describe a specific alignment observation, and record follow-up without guessing the remedy.

This lesson in the book, page 58 ↗

A Read the situation

Correcting the claim about drawer D3

During a wardrobe review, carpenter Luis tells client Priya that all fronts are flush. Priya points out drawer D3: its left edge projects beyond the neighboring front. Luis has not assessed the cause or remedy. He must correct his earlier statement and add the specific observation for follow-up. The conversation does not establish a measured offset, a tolerance failure, a damaged runner, or an agreed repair and completion time.

Priya: Client / Luis: Carpenter

Check the facts · 3 questions

Choose the answer supported by the briefing. Do not assume that a proposed action has already happened.

1. Which earlier statement must Luis correct?

Answer and explanation

D. All fronts are flush

Luis claimed all fronts were flush, which conflicts with the specific visible exception.

2. What does Priya identify?

Answer and explanation

D. D3's left edge projects beyond the neighboring front

The supplied observation concerns D3's projecting left edge, without a measurement or cause.

3. What should Luis do next in the conversation?

Answer and explanation

A. Correct the claim and record the specific observation for follow-up

The supported response is an explicit correction and accurate follow-up record, not a guessed remedy.

B Find the words

Vocabulary and collocations

Luis must withdraw all fronts are flush because D3 provides a specific exception. The observation is a projecting left edge relative to the neighboring front. It does not establish the projection amount, applicable tolerance, underlying cause, or correct remedy.

24 field terms and natural word combinations
flush
Aligned in the same plane at the relevant surfaces.check whether the fronts are flush
drawer front
Visible face panel of a drawer.identify the drawer front
neighboring front
Adjacent visible face used as a comparison.compare the neighboring front
projecting edge
Edge extending beyond a stated reference surface.describe the projecting edge
left edge
Left-hand boundary of the identified component.locate the left edge
alignment
Relative positioning of components or surfaces.review the alignment
offset
Displacement between specified reference positions or surfaces.measure the offset
tolerance
Permitted variation under an applicable specification.check the applicable tolerance
snag
Item requiring attention during a completion or quality review.record a snag
snagging list
Record of items requiring follow-up before agreed closure.add to the snagging list
punch list
Common North American term for a list of completion or correction items.update the punch list
fit review
Examination of how components align or work together.conduct a fit review
appearance review
Examination of visible finish and presentation.record an appearance review
drawer runner
Mechanism or support guiding a drawer's movement.inspect the drawer runner
hinge
Joint allowing a door or panel to pivot.identify the hinge
adjustment
Change to a component's setting or position.assess the need for adjustment
remedy
Action intended to correct an established problem.assess the appropriate remedy
root cause
Underlying reason for an observed problem.investigate the root cause
correction of record
Amendment of an inaccurate earlier statement.make a correction of record
specific exception
Identified case that contradicts a general claim.acknowledge the specific exception
quality claim
Statement about the condition or standard of work.qualify the quality claim
follow-up item
Matter retained for further checking or action.record a follow-up item
slip matching
Placing consecutive veneer leaves alongside each other in the same face orientation.compare slip matching
closeout status
Whether review and required follow-up are complete.preserve the closeout status

Record the component identifier, side, and comparison surface so follow-up is specific. Do not convert projects into a claim that the drawer is broken, or a possible adjustment into an agreed repair. The item remains open pending the appropriate assessment.

C Notice the language

A direct correction protects trust

When a client identifies an exception to an overall claim, acknowledge it explicitly. Correct the claim and record the observable detail. Avoid defending the original wording, minimizing the concern without a standard, or promising a repair before the cause has been assessed.

16 useful phrases, grouped by purpose
Correct the earlier statement
I need to correct what I said about all the fronts being flush.
Acknowledge the exception
You are right to point out D3.
Name the component
The observation concerns drawer D3.
Locate the edge
Its left edge projects beyond the neighboring front.
Avoid a guessed measurement
I have not established the amount of the offset.
Avoid a tolerance claim
I have not checked the applicable tolerance.
Keep the cause open
The cause has not been assessed.
Keep the remedy open
I have not determined the appropriate remedy.
Avoid blaming hardware
I cannot call the runner defective from this observation alone.
Record the follow-up
I will add the specific item to the snagging list.
Preserve the comparison
The neighboring front is the reference in this observation.
Avoid minimizing
I will not dismiss it as acceptable without checking.
Avoid a repair promise
No repair method or completion time is agreed.
Limit the finding
This identifies D3, not every drawer in the wardrobe.
Read back the item
D3 left edge projects; cause and remedy unassessed.
Close honestly
The earlier overall claim is corrected and this item remains open.
6 language and precision notes
All
A universal claim contradicted by even one verified exception.
Projects beyond
Describes relative position without supplying a measured amount.
Left edge
Makes the observation more useful than saying the drawer looks wrong.
Unassessed
Keeps an observation separate from a technical conclusion.
Correct what I said
Directly withdraws an inaccurate statement rather than quietly changing the subject.
Open item
Acknowledgement and recording do not establish that correction is complete.

D Check your understanding

Choose the precise message

Choose one best answer using the case facts and the stated purpose.

1. Which opening corrects the earlier claim?

Answer and explanation

D. I need to correct that: D3's left edge is not flush with the neighboring front.

The opening explicitly withdraws the inaccurate overall claim and identifies the exception.

2. Which snag entry is precise?

Answer and explanation

D. D3 left edge projects beyond neighboring front; cause and remedy unassessed.

The entry preserves the component, side, reference surface, and unassessed cause and remedy.

3. Which conclusion is unsupported?

Answer and explanation

D. A replacement runner is definitely required.

No assessment establishes that the runner caused the projection or needs replacement.

4. What remains open after recording the item?

Answer and explanation

D. Cause, remedy, and any completion arrangement

Recording the observation does not determine the cause, repair, or completion timing.

Check explanations in the book, page 97 ↗

E Conversations

Correcting the claim about drawer D3

Extended conversation · 20 turns · 10 gaps

Priya: Client / Luis: Carpenter

Complete the numbered gaps. Use each bank entry once, without changing its form.

Word bank
  • tolerance
  • remedy
  • correction of record
  • snagging list
  • drawer front
  • closeout status
  • quality claim
  • follow-up item
  • root cause
  • offset
  1. Priya
    Luis, you said all the fronts were flush. D3's left edge is sitting forward of the neighboring front. Can you check what you told me?
  2. Luis
    You're right; I need a 1. All fronts are flush was too broad. D3 is an exception, and I withdraw that overall statement.
  3. Priya
    Thank you. I don't want the list to say wardrobe problem, though. Someone coming back needs to know which edge we're discussing.
  4. Luis
    I'll identify the 2 as D3 and specify its left edge relative to the neighboring front. That is the visible difference.
  5. Priya
    Is it a runner fault? I wondered whether adjusting the hardware would bring it back, but I don't know what's behind it.
  6. Luis
    The 3 hasn't been assessed. I can't call the runner defective or promise that a particular adjustment will correct the position.
  7. Priya
    How far does it project? I can see the edge, but I haven't measured it, and I don't want my estimate repeated as a measurement.
  8. Luis
    The 4 isn't established numerically. I'll record the observation without inventing a millimeter figure or treating an estimate as a checked dimension.
  9. Priya
    So we can't call it within tolerance yet? Earlier I thought you were saying the whole installation had passed that comparison.
  10. Luis
    I haven't checked the applicable 5 for this item. I won't dismiss it as acceptable, or make the opposite specification finding, without that check.
  11. Priya
    Please make sure it stays on the follow-up list. I don't want a general handover tick to hide this particular point.
  12. Luis
    I'll add it to the 6: D3, left edge projects beyond the neighboring front, with cause and appropriate action still to be assessed.
  13. Priya
    Does correcting your earlier statement mean you're saying every drawer is wrong now? That's not what I'm asking you to report.
  14. Luis
    No. The 7 is narrowed by this specific exception. We have identified D3; we haven't established that all the other fronts have the same issue.
  15. Priya
    Can you tell me what will be done and when, or do those both have to wait for the follow-up assessment?
  16. Luis
    The 8 hasn't been determined, and no completion time is agreed. I'll avoid turning a possible adjustment into an approved repair promise.
  17. Priya
    Then read me the item as it will appear. I'd like the edge and comparison surface to survive the handoff.
  18. Luis
    The 9 says D3's left edge projects beyond the neighboring front; amount, cause, tolerance comparison, and appropriate action are not established.
  19. Priya
    That's accurate. Please keep it open after today's discussion. Correcting the wording doesn't itself put the front into the right position.
  20. Luis
    Agreed. The 10 stays open. I've corrected what I said, but the physical item and its follow-up still need to be resolved.

Answers and explanations
  1. correction of record Turn 2. Correction of record explicitly withdraws the inaccurate claim that all fronts are flush in light of the D3 observation.
  2. drawer front Turn 4. Drawer front is D3's visible face, the specific component Priya wants recorded rather than a vague whole-wardrobe concern.
  3. root cause Turn 6. Root cause remains unassessed; the visible projection does not establish a runner defect or another explanation.
  4. offset Turn 8. Offset is the amount of displacement between the fronts; no measured value is supplied by the visible observation alone.
  5. tolerance Turn 10. Tolerance is the permitted variation under the relevant specification; no comparison with that requirement has been completed here.
  6. snagging list Turn 12. Snagging list records the specific D3 item for follow-up; it does not establish that every drawer has the same condition.
  7. quality claim Turn 14. Quality claim that all fronts were flush must be corrected rather than retained as a conflicting statement beside the new observation.
  8. remedy Turn 16. Remedy is the corrective action, which has not been determined; therefore no method or completion time can be promised here.
  9. follow-up item Turn 18. Follow-up item is the precise D3 left-edge projection, with cause and remedy left unassessed for the next review.
  10. closeout status Turn 20. Closeout status remains open for D3 because recording the observation and correcting a statement do not complete the necessary follow-up.
Read the complete conversation
  1. Priya: Luis, you said all the fronts were flush. D3's left edge is sitting forward of the neighboring front. Can you check what you told me?
  2. Luis: You're right; I need a correction of record. All fronts are flush was too broad. D3 is an exception, and I withdraw that overall statement.
  3. Priya: Thank you. I don't want the list to say wardrobe problem, though. Someone coming back needs to know which edge we're discussing.
  4. Luis: I'll identify the drawer front as D3 and specify its left edge relative to the neighboring front. That is the visible difference.
  5. Priya: Is it a runner fault? I wondered whether adjusting the hardware would bring it back, but I don't know what's behind it.
  6. Luis: The root cause hasn't been assessed. I can't call the runner defective or promise that a particular adjustment will correct the position.
  7. Priya: How far does it project? I can see the edge, but I haven't measured it, and I don't want my estimate repeated as a measurement.
  8. Luis: The offset isn't established numerically. I'll record the observation without inventing a millimeter figure or treating an estimate as a checked dimension.
  9. Priya: So we can't call it within tolerance yet? Earlier I thought you were saying the whole installation had passed that comparison.
  10. Luis: I haven't checked the applicable tolerance for this item. I won't dismiss it as acceptable, or make the opposite specification finding, without that check.
  11. Priya: Please make sure it stays on the follow-up list. I don't want a general handover tick to hide this particular point.
  12. Luis: I'll add it to the snagging list: D3, left edge projects beyond the neighboring front, with cause and appropriate action still to be assessed.
  13. Priya: Does correcting your earlier statement mean you're saying every drawer is wrong now? That's not what I'm asking you to report.
  14. Luis: No. The quality claim is narrowed by this specific exception. We have identified D3; we haven't established that all the other fronts have the same issue.
  15. Priya: Can you tell me what will be done and when, or do those both have to wait for the follow-up assessment?
  16. Luis: The remedy hasn't been determined, and no completion time is agreed. I'll avoid turning a possible adjustment into an approved repair promise.
  17. Priya: Then read me the item as it will appear. I'd like the edge and comparison surface to survive the handoff.
  18. Luis: The follow-up item says D3's left edge projects beyond the neighboring front; amount, cause, tolerance comparison, and appropriate action are not established.
  19. Priya: That's accurate. Please keep it open after today's discussion. Correcting the wording doesn't itself put the front into the right position.
  20. Luis: Agreed. The closeout status stays open. I've corrected what I said, but the physical item and its follow-up still need to be resolved.
Dialogue in the book, page 64 ↗
Three more conversations at work

Each short conversation has its own fictional situation and facts.

I overlooked drawer D3

A carpenter repairs an inaccurate statement during a wardrobe review.

  1. Carpenter: I said all the fronts were flush, but you've identified an exception at D3.
  2. Client: Yes, the left edge projects beyond the next front when viewed from here.
  3. Carpenter: You're right about that visible relationship. My earlier statement was too broad.
  4. Client: Can we put that exact location on the snag list rather than cabinet alignment?
  5. Carpenter: Certainly. I'll describe D3's left edge projecting beyond the neighboring front for review.
  6. Client: Do you know whether the drawer front needs replacing, or just an adjustment?
  7. Carpenter: I haven't established the cause or remedy. The joinery lead needs to review it.
  8. Client: They offered Thursday morning. Can you confirm that appointment without promising completion that day?
  9. Carpenter: Yes, I'll confirm the review appointment and leave the remedy and completion date open.
  10. Client: Good. That gives us a specific follow-up instead of another general statement about the wardrobe.
Level and plumb are different questions

A carpenter and client discuss wording in a finish-review note.

  1. Carpenter: Your review note says the tall panel isn't level. Which direction concerns you?
  2. Client: It seems to lean sideways when I compare its edge with the doorway.
  3. Carpenter: For that vertical relationship, plumb is the more precise term than level.
  4. Client: Thanks. I wasn't sure how to describe the leaning without using the wrong word.
  5. Carpenter: We can say the vertical edge appears out of plumb and request a check.
  6. Client: Does appears mean you're dismissing what I can see from the hall?
  7. Carpenter: No. It preserves your observation while distinguishing it from a completed measurement or assessment.
  8. Client: Then include my viewing position so the joinery lead can examine the same concern.
  9. Carpenter: I will. The review should address that panel, not every cabinet in the room.
  10. Client: That is the item I meant; the revised wording makes the concern much clearer.
The agreed sample covers sheen

A carpenter and owner compare a finished panel with an acceptance sample.

  1. Carpenter: The acceptance sample records sheen and color. Which feature differs on this installed panel?
  2. Owner: The sheen looks similar, but there's a grain patch I don't like near the handle.
  3. Carpenter: That is a separate appearance concern. The sample note doesn't establish a grain-pattern guarantee.
  4. Owner: I understand wood varies, but I'd still like someone to review the visible patch.
  5. Carpenter: Of course. I'll record its location and your concern without saying the sample settles it.
  6. Owner: Please don't mark the whole panel accepted just because the sheen matches our reference.
  7. Carpenter: I'll keep the grain concern open and distinguish it from the matched sheen.
  8. Owner: Could the supplier show what variation was described when we selected this material?
  9. Carpenter: Yes, I'll request that information for the review, alongside the panel and sample references.
  10. Owner: Then we can discuss the specific concern with the right information rather than argue over match.

F Say it

Two scenarios to practice

Correcting the claim about drawer D3
  1. Read turns 1-10; make Luis's correction explicit and identify D3's left edge.
  2. Switch roles for turns 11-20; distinguish the observed projection from a tolerance decision or repair promise.

On your own: read both roles. With a partner: swap roles after the first reading. Use the completed conversation to check any line you need to repeat.

Correct and record the exception

Complete the review exchange. Correct the universal claim, identify the projecting edge, and leave the cause and remedy unresolved.

Choose one reply for each gap, then read the four turns in order. Check the explanation after each choice.

1. Carpenter: "I need to correct my claim that all fronts are ___."

Answer and explanation

D. flush

Flush was the inaccurate overall claim contradicted by D3's projecting edge.

2. Client: "The specific drawer is ___."

Answer and explanation

B. D3

D3 identifies the drawer that needs the recorded follow-up.

3. Carpenter: "Its ___ edge projects beyond the neighboring front."

Answer and explanation

C. left

Left identifies the side of the visible projection in the case.

4. Client: "The cause and remedy remain ___."

Answer and explanation

C. unassessed

Neither cause nor remedy has been determined by the observation alone.

Read the completed exchange twice, switching roles. Keep the printed facts and professional responsibilities unchanged.

Build vocabulary and collocations — complete prompt

TASK: Retrieve occupational vocabulary
1. Select four terms from the reference. Explain each in plain English, give two natural collocations and quote or create a short workplace example. Label invented examples. Contrast each term with a plausible confusable term; acknowledge regional variation.
2. Present one role-labelled exchange with one missing word or phrase and four choices A-D. Exactly one choice must fit the stated facts and grammar. Stop and wait for my answer before revealing it.
3. Explain all four options, then invite me to read the completed exchange aloud. Offer a sentence frame for optional spoken practice, never an open-ended writing assignment.
4. Repeat for the other three terms, one question at a time. Vary the correct letter and the situation; preserve the reference facts. If a choice is ambiguous, replace the item.
5. Finish with two new four-choice collocation questions for later review, with a clearly separate answer key. Do not claim to schedule a reminder.

Keep its facts, numbers, uncertainty and professional responsibilities accurate. Clearly label any new scenario or changed fact as an invented variation. Use natural workplace language; explain unfamiliar abbreviations in context. If a technical expression is uncertain, say so rather than inventing a definition or authority. Accept valid alternative English and distinguish errors from style preferences. Do not claim to certify my language level. Lesson 07: Reviewing fit, appearance, and snagging
Original fictional case: During a wardrobe review, carpenter Luis tells client Priya that all fronts are flush. Priya points out drawer D3: its left edge projects beyond the neighboring front. Luis has not assessed the cause or remedy. He must correct his earlier statement and add the specific observation for follow-up. The conversation does not establish a measured offset, a tolerance failure, a damaged runner, or an agreed repair and completion time.
Language workshop: Correcting the claim about drawer D3
Communication goal: Withdraw an inaccurate overall claim, describe a specific alignment observation, and record follow-up without guessing the remedy.
Language guidance: Luis must withdraw all fronts are flush because D3 provides a specific exception. The observation is a projecting left edge relative to the neighboring front. It does not establish the projection amount, applicable tolerance, underlying cause, or correct remedy.
Useful expressions (preserve the supplied facts): I need to correct what I said about all the fronts being flush. / You are right to point out D3. / The observation concerns drawer D3. / Its left edge projects beyond the neighboring front. / I have not established the amount of the offset. / I have not checked the applicable tolerance. / The cause has not been assessed. / I have not determined the appropriate remedy. / I cannot call the runner defective from this observation alone. / I will add the specific item to the snagging list. / The neighboring front is the reference in this observation. / I will not dismiss it as acceptable without checking. / No repair method or completion time is agreed. / This identifies D3, not every drawer in the wardrobe. / D3 left edge projects; cause and remedy unassessed. / The earlier overall claim is corrected and this item remains open.
Vocabulary: flush: Aligned in the same plane at the relevant surfaces. / drawer front: Visible face panel of a drawer. / neighboring front: Adjacent visible face used as a comparison. / projecting edge: Edge extending beyond a stated reference surface. / left edge: Left-hand boundary of the identified component. / alignment: Relative positioning of components or surfaces. / offset: Displacement between specified reference positions or surfaces. / tolerance: Permitted variation under an applicable specification. / snag: Item requiring attention during a completion or quality review. / snagging list: Record of items requiring follow-up before agreed closure. / punch list: Common North American term for a list of completion or correction items. / fit review: Examination of how components align or work together. / appearance review: Examination of visible finish and presentation. / drawer runner: Mechanism or support guiding a drawer's movement. / hinge: Joint allowing a door or panel to pivot. / adjustment: Change to a component's setting or position. / remedy: Action intended to correct an established problem. / root cause: Underlying reason for an observed problem. / correction of record: Amendment of an inaccurate earlier statement. / specific exception: Identified case that contradicts a general claim. / quality claim: Statement about the condition or standard of work. / follow-up item: Matter retained for further checking or action. / slip matching: Placing consecutive veneer leaves alongside each other in the same face orientation. / closeout status: Whether review and required follow-up are complete.
Speaking task: Read turns 1-10; make Luis's correction explicit and identify D3's left edge. Switch roles for turns 11-20; distinguish the observed projection from a tolerance decision or repair promise. Check the transfer, then read it with the neighboring front named as the comparison.
Partner: Priya: Client / Luis: Carpenter
Guided response task: Select supplied answers for the dialogue gaps. Check the explanations, then read the completed exchange aloud; no original writing is required.
Optional second-round challenge: Complete the review exchange. Correct the universal claim, identify the projecting edge, and leave the cause and remedy unresolved.
Model for comparison AFTER my attempt, not a response to give me first: Priya: Then read me the item as it will appear. I'd like the edge and comparison surface to survive the handoff.
Luis: The follow-up item says D3's left edge projects beyond the neighboring front; amount, cause, tolerance comparison, and appropriate action are not established.
Priya: That's accurate. Please keep it open after today's discussion. Correcting the wording doesn't itself put the front into the right position.
Luis: Agreed. The closeout status stays open. I've corrected what I said, but the physical item and its follow-up still need to be resolved.

Rehearse with an AI colleague — complete prompt

Copy the full text below into your preferred AI. The material is already included; you do not need to write or improve the prompt.

TASK: Interactive workplace role-play
1. Use the supplied case or dialogue as the starting situation. Give a two-sentence briefing and offer two relevant professional roles for me to choose from. For a supplied dialogue, use its actual roles. Ask which role I want, then stop and wait. Do not write my replies.
2. After I choose, identify who you will play and the immediate communication goal. Play the other professional; where a meeting requires a third person, label each of your speakers clearly. Start with one natural workplace turn and wait for my reply. Keep most turns to one to three sentences and ask no more than one question at a time.
3. Let the exchange develop across six to ten learner turns, or end earlier if I type "feedback". Respond to what I actually say. Include a plausible clarification, disagreement or tradeoff without silently changing the starting facts. Mark any added constraint as a fictional second-round variation. Do not resolve approvals, evidence or commitments that remain uncertain.
4. Stay in role during the exchange. If my meaning is unclear, ask for clarification naturally. Give a hint only if I ask or cannot proceed. Do not deliver a model conversation in advance.
5. At the debrief, quote two of my phrases: one successful choice and one worth improving. Check factual accuracy, language, register and whether the next step was clear. Give up to three focused suggestions. Ask me to retry the weakest turn; wait. Only then offer an alternative wording and a harder replay.
Use voice replies when available. If I prefer selections, offer four plausible spoken replies and explain my choice before continuing. Do not require written compositions.

08Handing over records and return visitsDocuments received, one cover outstanding+

Your goal

Confirm supplied documents, record an undelivered part, and distinguish an accepted callback commitment from an unconfirmed return visit.

This lesson in the book, page 67 ↗

A Read the situation

Documents received, one cover outstanding

At a cabinet handover, owner Daniel receives the cabinet drawings and the correct finish care sheet from joinery lead Mina. A replacement shelf pin cover is due but has not arrived. Mina accepts responsibility for supplier follow-up and commits to calling Daniel on Friday. No return-visit date is confirmed. Warranty terms remain those in the supplied document; Mina must not invent wider coverage or imply that receiving the documents means every outstanding item is complete.

Daniel: Owner / Mina: Joinery lead

Check the facts · 3 questions

Choose the answer supported by the briefing. Do not assume that a proposed action has already happened.

1. Which documents does Daniel receive?

Answer and explanation

C. Cabinet drawings and the correct finish care sheet

The case confirms delivery of the cabinet drawings and the correct care sheet.

2. What remains outstanding?

Answer and explanation

C. A replacement shelf pin cover that is due but not delivered

The replacement cover has not arrived, so that item remains open.

3. What does Mina commit to?

Answer and explanation

B. Supplier follow-up and a Friday call, with no confirmed return visit

Mina owns supplier follow-up and promises the call, not a visit or delivery date.

B Find the words

Vocabulary and collocations

The drawings and correct care sheet are received. The replacement cover is not. Mina accepts supplier follow-up and a Friday call, but no visit date is confirmed. Unlike a requested callback, the Friday call is an explicit commitment in this case.

24 field terms and natural word combinations
handover pack
Collection of documents supplied at a project handover.confirm the handover pack
cabinet drawing
Drawing showing the relevant cabinet arrangement or details.provide the cabinet drawings
care sheet
Instructions for looking after a specified finish or product.supply the correct care sheet
finish identification
Confirmation of the surface treatment a document applies to.check finish identification
shelf pin
Small support component used to locate or carry a shelf.identify the shelf pin
pin cover
Cover associated with the specified pin or fitting.identify the replacement pin cover
replacement part
Component supplied to replace a specified item.track the replacement part
outstanding item
Matter not yet completed, delivered, or resolved.retain an outstanding item
due delivery
Expected arrival that has not necessarily occurred.distinguish due delivery from receipt
supplier follow-up
Contact with the supplier about an unresolved matter.accept supplier follow-up
action owner
Person responsible for carrying out a defined next step.name the action owner
callback commitment
Promise to make a return call at a stated time.confirm the callback commitment
return visit
Later attendance at the property.arrange a return visit
appointment confirmation
Explicit agreement of a visit arrangement.await appointment confirmation
warranty document
Written terms describing the applicable warranty.refer to the warranty document
coverage
Matters included under the stated warranty terms.verify the documented coverage
exclusion
Matter outside the applicable terms or scope.check a stated exclusion
document receipt
Confirmation that a document has been received.record document receipt
part receipt
Confirmation that a component has physically arrived.verify part receipt
fitting status
Whether the relevant part has been installed or fitted.confirm fitting status
handover record
Note of documents, completed work, and unresolved items at transfer.update the handover record
open-item tracker
Record used to follow unresolved matters to completion.update the open-item tracker
commitment boundary
Limit of what has actually been promised.preserve the commitment boundary
closure evidence
Information supporting that an item is genuinely complete.obtain closure evidence

Keep warranty wording tied to the supplied document instead of paraphrasing it into a broader promise. Receipt of documents does not prove that the replacement part has arrived, been fitted, or closed. No supplier delivery date or return appointment is supplied.

C Notice the language

Close completed items without hiding the remainder

A useful handover can acknowledge documents received while retaining an outstanding part and its follow-up. Name the responsible person and the precise commitment. A promise to call is valuable on its own and should not be expanded into a promised visit or changed warranty.

16 useful phrases, grouped by purpose
Confirm the documents
You have the cabinet drawings and the correct finish care sheet.
Identify the open item
The replacement shelf pin cover is still outstanding.
State receipt accurately
It is due, but it has not been delivered.
Accept ownership
I will follow up with the supplier.
Name the owner
Mina owns that supplier follow-up.
Make the stated commitment
I will call you on Friday.
Separate call and visit
Friday is the callback commitment, not a confirmed return visit.
Keep attendance open
The return-visit date is not confirmed.
Avoid a delivery promise
I do not have a confirmed arrival date for the cover.
Use the supplied terms
The warranty remains as stated in the supplied document.
Avoid wider coverage
I am not adding a new warranty promise verbally.
Keep records separate
Document receipt does not close the outstanding part.
Preserve fitting status
I will not record an undelivered part as fitted.
Read back the handover
Documents received, cover outstanding, supplier follow-up with Mina, Friday call.
Avoid premature closure
The cover item stays open pending the actual outcome.
Close with a clear boundary
The call is committed; delivery and return attendance are not yet confirmed.
6 language and precision notes
Due versus delivered
Expected arrival and actual receipt are different statuses.
I will call
An explicit commitment, stronger than a request for someone to call.
Friday
Applies to the phone call, not automatically to delivery or site attendance.
As stated
Refers back to the supplied warranty wording without changing it.
Outstanding
Keeps the unresolved cover visible after other handover items are complete.
Received versus fitted
Physical arrival and installation are separate stages.

D Check your understanding

Choose the precise message

Choose one best answer using the case facts and the stated purpose.

1. Which handover summary is correct?

Answer and explanation

D. Drawings and care sheet received; cover outstanding; Mina follows up and calls Friday; visit unconfirmed.

The summary preserves completed documents, the open part, ownership, call, and unconfirmed visit.

2. What does Mina's Friday commitment cover?

Answer and explanation

B. A phone call to Daniel

Mina explicitly commits to calling Friday, while delivery and attendance remain unconfirmed.

3. Which warranty statement is appropriate?

Answer and explanation

D. The terms remain those in the supplied document.

The case authorizes no change to the supplied warranty terms in either direction.

4. What is insufficient evidence to close the cover item?

Answer and explanation

D. Receipt of the drawings and care sheet alone

Receiving documents does not establish delivery, fitting, or resolution of the replacement cover.
